🟡 **H297** construction-time `mkdir` side effects -- managers create system dirs at `__init__`/`_refresh` with no cap gate/opt-in (`misc_data_manager.py:589` `/usr/share/misc`; `bsd_compat:101`; `dict:171`; `games_data:120-121`; `dconf:73-75`; `icc_profile:111-112`; `nls:86`; `ppd:110`; `tmac:106`; `user_help:127`; `terminfo:111`; `wayland:102-103`; `xdg:73-74`; `xml:93`) -- hidden side effects, raise off non-root Linux, wrong-place trees off Linux; 🟡 **H298** fail-open broad `except Exception: return False` around privileged ops (`sendmail_manager`, `misc_data_manager`, `bsd_compat:220,235`, `games_data:229,244`, `xml:213,228`) -- security-relevant failures silently swallowed; 🟡 **H299** **H7 missing GPL-3.0 header cluster** -- only 3/61 (`kernel_source_manager`, `rpm_manager`, `source_manager`) carry any licence/Copyright/SPDX string, **58/61 ship no licence at all** (largest missing-header cluster in the tree); 🟡 **H300** hardcoded absolute `/usr/...` system paths baked into class constants (not runtime/arg-resolved), combined with import-time `mkdir` = non-portable + wrong-place creation. 💭 **H301** SIMULATED privileged/kernel-surface modules masquerade as functional -- `lib_manager`/`sbin_manager`/`rpm_manager`/`gcc_manager`/`x11_manager` (privileged FHS managers, in-memory only) + kernel-interface modules (`io_uring`,`landlock`,`seccomp`,`ntsync`,`iommufd`,`dmabuf`,`vduse`,`tee`,`netlink`,`procfs`,`sysfs`,`syscalls`) define constants/enums but have NO real syscall wiring (no `io_uring_setup`/`landlock_create_ruleset`/etc.) -- they imply OS access that doesn't exist (false-state risk, like sbin H235); 💭 **H302** per-file baseline gaps -- many modules lack `from __future__ import annotations`/module docstring/`__all__`/type hints, use lazy `import shutil` inside functions, and `usr/` ships **no `_selftest()`/test module at all**. **Fix order**: GPL headers on 58 modules (H7 H299) -> capability-gate LIVE privileged ops (H296) + move `mkdir` out of `__init__` behind explicit bootstrap (H297) -> runtime/arg-resolve `/usr` roots (H300) -> surface/avoid fail-open `except` (H298) -> label simulated managers + wire or clearly mark kernel-surface modules (H301) -> add baseline + `_selftest` (H302).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] `var/` study notes (v1.43).** `var/` = **4 Python modules (~706 LOC) + 2 data files (`lib/umeros/consent_ledger.json`, `log/umeros_initrd.log`)** -- the FHS `/var` variable-data hierarchy manager (`LogManager` @ `/var/log`, `SpoolManager` @ `/var/spool`, `VarDirectoryManager` @ `/var/local,/var/lock,/var/opt,/var/run,/var/tmp`). **Smallest LIVE folder studied, and 100% LIVE** (every manager does real FS writes -- no SIMULATED modules, unlike `usr/`). Dangerous-call surface clean (ZERO `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec`/`tarfile`/`zipfile`/`ctypes`/`sys.path`); the only destructive call is `shutil.rmtree`x3, but all real FS writes are fed by **unsanitized path params** (the headline risk). NO `CapabilityManager` gate anywhere; NO construction-time `mkdir` (on-demand, scoped -- better than `usr/` H297). **Findings: 1 🔴 + 3 🟡 + 1 💭**. 🔴 **H303** **path-traversal across all three Var managers** -- `name`/`username`/`directory`/`filename` are joined as `self.<x>_path / param` with no scoping/`realpath` check; `Path` collapses `..`, so `remove_local_item("../../etc/passwd")`→`unlink("/etc/passwd")`, `VarDirectoryManager.remove_local_item("../etc")`→`shutil.rmtree("/etc")`, and `SpoolManager.set_cron_user("../../etc/cron.d/x", jobs)`→writes `/etc/cron.d/x` = **cron RCE as root**; `LogManager.write_log("../../etc/cron.d/x", …)` = arbitrary append (CWE-22). 🟡 **H304** privileged FS ops (write/append/unlink/rmtree/rename) with **no `CapabilityManager` gate** (cap-gate family H227/H233/H267/H273/H281/H283/H296); 🟡 **H305** fail-open broad `except Exception: return False/None` (bare `except Exception: pass` in `directory_manager.py:154`) swallows security-relevant failures; 🟡 **H306** H7 **Apache-2.0 strays** -- `directory_manager.py:14`/`spool_manager.py:13`/`log_manager.py:13` declare `Licence: GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)` (conflicts GPL-3.0 canonical; `__init__.py` has no header -- opposite of `usr/`'s missing-header H299). 💭 **H307** `var/` ships **no `_selftest()`/test module**; baseline otherwise good (`from __future__`, type hints, docstrings, logging) but uses lazy `import shutil`/`import gzip` inside functions. **Note:** `var/lib/umeros/consent_ledger.json` is a real persistence artifact (links to the `legal/` consent ledger, H128-H141) -- worth a cross-folder check that the ledger is written/read under a capability + not world-readable. **Fix order**: validate/normalize path params (reject `..`, `realpath` under the manager root) -- H303 → capability-gate all privileged FS ops (H304) → surface fail-open `except` (H305) → GPL-3.0 headers (H7 H306) → add `test_var.py` (H307).

### 4.5 Performance 🟡

- [ ] **No N+1 / repeated work** in loops (e.g. IPC send-in-loop, fs lookups).
- [ ] **Compression tests use compressible data** (`b"Umer OS test pattern " * N`), never `os.urandom()` — LZMA on random data times out (per dev guide).
- [ ] **Async correctness** — `await` coroutines; no blocking calls on the event loop; `sched.stop()` awaited in teardown; `asyncio.Lock` guards shared queues (per scheduler design).
- [ ] **No unbounded growth** — caches/maps bounded or sized.

### 4.6 Testing 🔴/🟡

- [ ] **Behavioral change has tests.** New command → add to its suite + a `_selftest()` entry; new kernel/security/quantum module → suite tests (see `tests/` mappings).
- [ ] **Tests are hermetic & fast** — no real network/host dependence unless explicitly a container/integration test; skip (don't hang) on missing platform (pattern from `liboqs/test_leaks.py`).
- [ ] **Assert the contract** — return codes, raises, HMAC verify, capability allow/deny, signature check — not just "runs without error."
- [ ] **No regressions** — full suite green (921) before merge.
- [ ] **Flaky-test check** — tests don't depend on wall-clock, ordering, or global state.

### 4.7 Documentation & process 💭/🟡

- [ ] **PR description** explains *what* and *why*; references an issue if applicable.
- [ ] **Public API/docstring updates** included; `docs/` regenerated if API changed.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] License consistency** — headers/comments don't contradict GPL-3.0 (canonical, per LICENSE/setup.py/README); fix the Apache-2.0 strays in `developer_guide.md` and master prompts (H7).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] UI-tech consistency** — Flutter (Dart) is canonical (decided 2026-08-20). Remove/refresh Kivy references from `README.md`/`setup.py`/`requirements.txt` as legacy; treat any new Kivy-only UI code as legacy (H11, H15, §1.1).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] README accuracy** — PRs that *match* README's documented structure are fine, but reviewers must not reject code because README's "Project Structure" or code examples disagree with the real repo (README is aspirational; see H14). When a PR changes public APIs, the matching README example/section should be updated or flagged in the PR.
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Contributing-tooling alignment** — README tells contributors to `pip install black flake8 mypy pylint` and run them, but those are commented out of `requirements.txt` and not enforced. Canonical tooling = **Ruff + Mypy** per §7; update README's "Contributing"/"Development Setup" to match (H9/H15).
- [ ] **Changelog / tier migration** noted when moving code `EXPERIMENTAL`→`TODAY`.

---

### 4.8 Frontend — Flutter/Dart & HCI principles 🟡

The frontend lives in **`ui/flutter_ui/`** and is written in **Dart** (Flutter). It must follow established **Human-Computer Interaction (HCI)** principles as a design mandate (the user explicitly requires HCI adherence). The current UI already does well: Material 3 theming (light/dark), `tooltip:` on icon buttons (a11y), keyboard shortcuts (Ctrl+K search, Esc to dismiss), a responsive windowing model (drag/resize/snap), notifications, and a spotlight search — good visibility, flexibility, and recognition-over-recall. Review new frontend PRs against these heuristics:

- [ ] **[HCI] Visibility of system status.** Show loading/working states; confirm async actions (OTA, package install, network calls) where they happen; surface errors in-context. (Nielsen #1)
- [ ] **[HCI] Match the real world / plain language.** Avoid OS-internal jargon in user-facing labels — e.g. "CPUIdle & Governor" / "Power & Idle" are developer terms; prefer "Power & Performance". (Nielsen #2)
- [ ] **[HCI] User control & freedom.** Every destructive or mode-changing action has an undo/escape path: window close/min/max are present; destructive ops (file delete, package remove) must confirm or be reversible. (Nielsen #3)
- [x] **[HCI] Consistency & standards.** Use Material 3 components consistently; **app registry is already centralised** — `app_registry.dart` `AppRegistry` is the single source of truth, consumed by `_DesktopGrid`/`_GlobalSearchModal`/`_LaunchPad` (in `desktop_shell.dart`) + `dock.dart` (resolved Hotspot H23, session 47; no duplicate lists remain). (Nielsen #4)
- [ ] **[HCI] Error prevention.** Confirm before destructive actions; validate inputs (e.g. the Control Center custom-wallpaper path field). (Nielsen #5)
- [ ] **[HCI] Recognition over recall.** Keep LaunchPad, Dock, and spotlight search; don't force GUI users to memorise commands. (Nielsen #6)
- [ ] **[HCI] Flexibility & efficiency.** Preserve keyboard shortcuts + draggable/resizable windows; expose accelerators for power users. (Nielsen #7)
- [ ] **[HCI] Aesthetic & minimalist.** M3 already applied; avoid clutter, keep one primary action per surface. (Nielsen #8)
- [ ] **[HCI] Help & documentation.** GUI surfaces self-explanatory; keep the About/Help entry and add first-run hints. (Nielsen #10)
- [x] **[HCI] Accessibility (a11y).** Add `Semantics` labels for screen readers on custom widgets (dock, draggable windows, launchpad); ensure colour contrast in both themes; respect `uiScale`/`enableGlassmorphism`. Test with `find.bySemanticsLabel`, not just a mount smoke test (Hotspot H26). (Nielsen #9 + WCAG 2.1 AA)
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Frontend is Dart-only.** No Python/Kivy/Tkinter in the UX path. Legacy `ui/*.py` (Tkinter/CLI) is a retired headless fallback only (H25). Native Android platform code, if any, is Kotlin/Java (not the app logic).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Frontend egress respects privacy.** The Dart UI uses `http` + `webview_flutter_windows` (browser/network apps) — any call sending user data off-device must pass the same opt-in consent gate as the backend (`AIGovernance`, §4.3 / H18).

### 4.9 Build & release integrity 🟡

`build/` is the **PyInstaller output** for the `UmerOS-GUI` freeze. Of its 16 files (~42 MB), only **2 are authored**: `build/UmerOS-GUI.spec` (PyInstaller spec) and `build/__init__.py` (empty package marker). Everything under `build/UmerOS-GUI/` — `*.toc`, `PYZ-00.pyz`, `UmerOS-GUI.pkg`, `base_library.zip`, `localpycs/*.pyc`, `warn-*.txt`, `xref-*.html` — is **generated** and correctly **out of review scope** per §0 (generated `.pyz`/`.exe` artifacts). The reviewable surface is the `.spec`. Build/release PRs must satisfy:

- [ ] **[UmerOS] Build targets the canonical frontend.** `build/UmerOS-GUI.spec:5` freezes `ui/umeros_gui.py` — the **legacy Tkinter GUI that H25 already retired** as superseded by Flutter (`ui/flutter_ui/`). Shipping this binary ships the wrong (superseded) frontend. Repoint the build at the canonical frontend before any release; don't distribute the retired Tkinter app as "UmerOS" (Hotspot H41).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Frozen artifacts are signed.** `build/UmerOS-GUI.spec:36` sets `codesign_identity=None` — the distributable is **unsigned**, which breaks the zero-trust signed-artifact mandate (§4.2, signed `.umerpkg`, H17/H27/H28). Sign the frozen binary + `.pkg` before distribution; reconsider `upx=True` (L29), which packs/obscures the payload and trips AV (Hotspot H42).
- [ ] **[UmerOS] Build is reproducible / portable.** The spec hardcodes an absolute Windows path `UmerOS\ui\umeros_gui.py` (L5) — machine-specific, breaks in CI/other dev machines. Derive the entry from the repo root so the build runs anywhere (Hotspot H43).
- [x] **[UmerOS] Complete, optimized freeze.** `datas=[]`/`binaries=[]`/`hiddenimports=[]`/`excludes=[]` and `optimize=0` (L8-15) declare no assets/runtime deps and ship unoptimized bytecode — risk of missing data files / Tk runtime on a clean machine. Declare needed `datas`/`binaries`/`hiddenimports`, set `optimize>=1`, and verify the frozen app runs from a clean VM (Hotspot H44).  **RESOLVED (session 62):** `optimize=1`; empty `datas`/`binaries`/`hiddenimports` are intentional (thin stdlib-only Flutter host).
- [x] **[UmerOS] Build is codified.** The build is invoked ad-hoc (`pyinstaller UmerOS-GUI.spec`); there is **no `Makefile`/`build.py`/CI build step** in-repo (extends H16 — no CI test step either). Codify the build + a signed-artifact release job so it is reproducible and auditable (Hotspot H44).  **RESOLVED (session 62):** `build/build_umeros_gui.py` codifies the freeze + mandatory signed-artifact gate.
- [x] **[UmerOS] Spec carries a license/tier label.** `build/UmerOS-GUI.spec` has no license header or `[TODAY]/[EXPERIMENTAL]/[FUTURE]` label (extends H7/H30/H40); `build/__init__.py` is a harmless 0-byte marker. Add a header to the spec for consistency (Hotspot H45).

---

## 5. The review process (workflow)

```
 Author dev → self-check (§4) → open PR → CI gates (§7) → ≥1 reviewer →
 resolve 🔴, waive 🟡 (maintainer) → approval → squash-merge to main →
 post-merge: confirm green, update docs
```

1. **Author self-check.** Run §4 against the diff locally. Run `ruff`, `bandit -c pyproject.toml`, `mypy`, and the relevant `tests/` suite.
2. **Open PR.** Target `main`. Fill the PR template (Section 10). Link issue. Keep it focused. Mark the PR's own tier.
3. **CI gates block merge** until green (lint, type, security baseline, full test suite, coverage floor).
4. **Assign reviewer(s).** At least one human reviewer; security-sensitive PRs (auth, IPC, crypto, `subprocess`, `eval`, self-healing/auto-patch, package signing, installer) require a **security reviewer** sign-off.
5. **Review round.** Use 🔴/🟡/💭 inline. One complete pass, not drip-fed. Ask when intent is unclear rather than assuming wrong.
6. **Address.** Author fixes 🔴, responds to each 🟡 (fix or justify). Re-request review.
7. **Approve & merge.** Maintainer merges (squash) only when §8 criteria are met.
8. **Post-merge.** Verify CI on `main` is green; update `docs/`/README test counts if they changed.

**SLA (suggested):** author responds within 2 business days; reviewer first pass within 2 business days.

---

## 6. Roles & responsibilities

| Role                  | Responsibility                                                                                                                       |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Author**            | Small focused PR; passes §4 self-check & all CI; addresses every comment; keeps suite green                                          |
| **Reviewer**          | Domain + standards check; at least one 🔴/🟡 pass; approves only when comfortable owning the merge                                   |
| **Security reviewer** | Sign-off on auth/IPC/crypto/sandbox/`subprocess`/`eval`/self-healing/package-signing/installer PRs; verifies no secret leakage       |
| **Maintainer**        | Enforces merge criteria (§8); waives 🟡 with rationale; protects `main`; curates baselines; resolves design inconsistencies (H7/H11) |

---

## 7. Automation & CI (build on what exists)

The repo already runs `.github/workflows/security_scan.yml` (Bandit, Safety, Trivy, ZAP). **Extend it; do not start from scratch.**

**Add to the pipeline (most are already in `requirements.txt` but commented out):**

- **Formatter/linter:** adopt **Ruff** (`ruff format` + `ruff check`) — fast, single tool. Add a `pyproject.toml` `[tool.ruff]` config. This replaces the commented `black`/`flake8` lines.
- **Type checker:** **Mypy** in non-strict (or gradual) mode; zero new `Any` in security-critical modules.
- **Static security:** keep **Bandit**, but scope it and baseline it:
  - Run `bandit -c pyproject.toml -r .` with `exclude` for `Old Linux Code/,node_modules/,*/__pycache__/,tests/` so the C reference tree stops creating noise.
  - Generate a baseline (`bandit -f json > bandit-baseline.json`) and fail only on *new* findings (use `bandit-baseline` diff) so we don't block on pre-existing debt.
- **Secret scanning:** add **gitleaks** (or `trufflehog`) as a pre-commit + CI step to catch the next `sk-or-v1-…` before it lands. Pre-commit should also run Ruff + a `.gitignore` guard.
- **Dependency scan:** keep **Safety**; add **pip-audit** as a cross-check. 🔴 **`g4f>=0.4.0`** (free GPT-4o/Claude via reverse-engineered endpoints, no API key) is a supply-chain **and** provider-ToS risk — do **not** rely on it for production paths; if used at all, vendor it, pin it, and isolate it behind a capability (Hotspot H15). **Pin everything**: replace floating `>=` with `==`/`~=` and add hashes; the unbounded `>` on `fastapi`/`uvicorn`/`httpx` can pull breaking majors. **Remove `kivy`** from `requirements.txt` (it is already commented out at line 52) **and from `setup.py` `install_requires` (line 15)** — Flutter (Dart) is now canonical (H11/§1.1), so Kivy is legacy drift to retire, not re-add (H15/H22).
- **Run the suite (currently missing — see H16):** add a CI step that executes the whole `tests/` tree. Standardize on **pytest** with a `pyproject.toml [tool.pytest]` config (`testpaths = tests`, `pythonpath = .`, `asyncio_mode = auto`) so it collects both the `unittest.TestCase` files and `tests/quantum/`. Pin `pytest>=8` (the bundled pytest imports the removed `imp` module and fails on Python 3.12+). With this, the "full suite green" gate in §8 becomes enforceable.
- **Coverage gate:** enforce a floor (e.g. new lines ≥ 80% covered) via `pytest-cov`; publish the report.
- **Pin actions** with commit SHAs (current `actions/checkout@v3` etc. are floating); the ZAP step targets `localhost:8000` — only include it when an API server actually runs in CI, or it's a no-op.
- **Pre-commit:** `.pre-commit-config.yaml` running Ruff + gitleaks + trailing-whitespace/EOF on every local commit.

> Keep CI fast: run lint+type+unit in parallel; reserve Trivy/ZAP for `main`/release, not every PR.

---

## 8. Merge criteria — Definition of Done

A PR may merge **only when ALL** are true:

- [ ] 🔴 items resolved; 🟡 either fixed or waived in writing by a maintainer.
- [ ] **Tier label present** on the PR and on new modules/features.
- [ ] CI green: Ruff clean, Mypy clean (new code), Bandit no *new* findings vs baseline, full suite (~1880) passes, coverage floor met (H9 gates in place; suite-green tracked under H16).
- [ ] At least one approval; security PRs have security-reviewer approval.
- [ ] No secrets committed (gitleaks clean); `.gitignore` covers `settings.local.json` and `*secret*`.
- [ ] Tests included for behavioral change; docs/API updated if changed.
- [ ] PR description complete; linked issue closed/updated.

---

New module-level `_version_key()` parses dotted-decimal versions (`"1.10.0"`, `"v2.1"`, `"1"`) into an integer tuple — trailing zero components normalised so `"1.0"` == `"1.0.0"` == `"1"` — and the decision is: byte-identical strings → up-to-date; both parse → update only when `new_key > cur_key` (a semantic downgrade is still refused); either side unparseable → **reinstall with a loud WARNING** rather than silently skip (the reinstall is idempotent, sits behind the same `CAP_FS_ADMIN` gate, and self-heals a corrupt DB version entry). `packaging` was **not** used: setup.py declares only numpy + cryptography, so the comparator is self-contained stdlib rather than a new runtime dependency (the row's suggested remedy). | H219 | 🟡 | `quantum/qkd.py:354-383` `key_reconciliation` | **Toy/wrong QKD error reconciliation** - the function simply flips Bob's bits to match Alice (`reconciled_bob[i] = 1 - reconciled_bob[i]`) instead of performing error reconciliation; this forces QBER to ~0 and defeats the entire security argument of BB84/E91 (an eavesdropper is never detected). | Implement real reconciliation (e.g. Cascade/BICONF) over the sifted key; keep the QBER threshold check meaningful. |
  ✅ RESOLVED (session 207, REAL FIX + DRIFT-CORRECTED): premise **TRUE**, line refs stale (`key_reconciliation` was at **367-396**, not `:354-383`). It was genuine **oracle reconciliation** — a per-bit loop `if reconciled_alice[i] != reconciled_bob[i]: reconciled_bob[i] = 1 - reconciled_bob[i]`, which requires Alice's secret key. Nothing in the repo called it (only the `quantum/__init__.py` and `__all__` export lists) and there was no QKD test file. One nuance: `run()` computes QBER **before** reconciliation (`errors = sum(a != b for a, b in zip(sifted_alice, sifted_bob))`), so an eavesdropper *was* still detected there — the row overstated that half — but the exported function was bogus for any caller. **Fix:** replaced with a real **Cascade** implementation. (1) `cascade_reconcile(alice, bob, *, block_size=4, passes=4, seed=None) -> ReconciliationResult` — each pass partitions the key into blocks, exchanges **block parities only** (`_xor_block`), and for any block whose parity differs binary-searches down to a single bit (`_binary_locate_and_flip`, comparing sub-block parities only, never Alice's individual bits) which Bob flips; after pass 0 the key is randomly permuted so even-error (parity-invisible) blocks get re-split; block size doubles per pass; stops early when a pass corrects nothing. (2) `ReconciliationResult` exposes `alice_key`/`bob_key`/`corrected_bits`/`parity_bits_leaked`/`passes_used`/`residual_errors` plus `leak_fraction`/`is_reconciled`; `residual_errors` is documented as a **simulator-only diagnostic** (in a real run neither party can compute it). (3) `key_reconciliation(...)` kept as a thin 2-tuple wrapper for API compatibility. (4) QBER threshold promoted to a named `QBER_SECURITY_THRESHOLD = 0.11` used by **both** BB84 and E91 instead of duplicated magic numbers, satisfying "keep the QBER threshold check meaningful", and exported. (5) Module docstring rewritten to explain parity-only reconciliation, explicitly disown oracle behaviour, and carry the §1 Author/License trailer. Pinned by `tests/test_qkd_cascade_h219.py` (20 passed): source invariants (oracle line gone; **AST** check that neither function compares both keys per-index; parity helpers present; named threshold used by both protocols with the bare `0.11` literals gone; docstring mentions "parit"/"oracle"; §1 trailers), behaviour (single error corrected; **decisive** `test_even_errors_are_not_corrected` — two errors inside one block leave the block parity unchanged so residual must stay 2, which no oracle could produce; 5 % QBER converges to 0 residual in 3 passes; Alice's key never modified; leak reported; tuple API; length mismatch raises; empty keys), and QBER-still-meaningful checks. Full run **4966 passed / 7 failed / 0 errors / 72 skipped** — the same 7 pre-existing external-rewrite failures as H218 (`test_legal_pii_h137` ×5, `test_lib_pam_h150`, `test_media_license_h160`); **zero new failures** (4946 + 20 new). |

Re-enabling it verbatim would have broken `docs/developer_guide.md:226-228` (`mac = auth.sign_message(...)`; `auth.verify_message({...}, mac)`), the GUI caller, and `tests/test_security.py::TestIPCAuthenticator`, which pins `sign_message -> str` **and** determinism (`test_sign_deterministic`: same message + same key => same MAC). (b) The row missed a second lie: the class docstring claimed **HMAC-SHA3-512** while the code hashes with `hashlib.sha3_256`; the module docstring claimed both "SHA3-512" (L35) and plain "HMAC-SHA256" (L21); and `ui/umeros_gui.py:1567` repeated the 512 claim to the user. **Remedy, chosen to keep the documented wire format:** replay defence moved into the *verifier*, where it needs no envelope. `verify_message` remembers every MAC it accepts for `replay_window` (default 300 s) and refuses a second presentation, so a captured `(message, MAC)` pair cannot be re-delivered to that instance; `_prune` bounds the cache by TTL **and** a hard `max_entries` cap (oldest half dropped under a flood - a documented bounded-memory fallback, not silent exhaustion). Crucially the MAC is compared **before** anything is remembered: otherwise a flood of *distinct* forged MACs would fill the cache and evict genuine entries, re-enabling replay of exactly those. `sign_envelope`/`verify_envelope` are the **opt-in** form for callers that need replay defence to survive a restart or hold across receiver instances - the envelope binds `timestamp`, `nonce` and `ttl` into the signed bytes, rejects stale *and* future-stamped envelopes, never mutates the caller's dict, and is total (malformed input returns `False` rather than raising, because envelopes arrive from untrusted peers). `replay_window=0` restores purely stateless verification. Both verify paths keep `hmac.compare_digest`; the SHA3-512 claims are corrected to SHA3-256 in the module docstring, the class docstring and the GUI. Pinned by `tests/test_ipc_replay_h247.py` (**29 passed, 15 subtests**): replay refused *for the state reason and not because the MAC broke* (the decisive `test_the_replay_is_refused_because_of_state_not_because_the_mac_broke`, which also proves a fresh instance still accepts that MAC - the honest per-instance boundary), a forged-MAC flood cannot evict genuine entries, 50 distinct messages are not mistaken for replays, `rotate_key` clears the cache, expiry via a fake clock, memory bound under a 40-message flood, the documented flood tradeoff, eight envelope behaviours (round-trip, no mutation, replay, tamper, stale, future-stamped, per-call uniqueness, survives-restart) and 15 malformed-envelope cases, plus the unchanged public API (64-char hex, determinism, the `developer_guide` snippet verbatim, tamper => False) and four claim-vs-code checks. Non-vacuity confirmed by **9 mutations, all caught** - replay check removed, compare/remember order inverted, `rotate_key` keeping the cache, future-timestamp check dropped, stale check dropped, envelope replay check removed, the SHA3-512 docstring lie restored, memory cap disabled, expiry disabled; each mutant asserted to differ from the original and the file restored byte-identically (sha256 verified). Existing `tests/test_security.py::TestIPCAuthenticator` (8) still green. Regression: **6125 testcases, 0 errors, 95 skipped, 6 failed** - the 5 known `test_legal_pii_h137` plus the pre-existing external `tests/test_layer_reachability.py` drift (`examples/__init__.py`), neither attributable to this row; the +29 delta is exactly the new file. Next: **H248** - 🟡 `security/security.py:328-350` `AIBehavioralMonitor` `analyze_action`/`_trigger_quarantine`: a hardcoded 4-entry rule set labelled "AI-driven", and a quarantine that only calls `log.critical` with no isolation. Say **'continues'** for H248.

| H287 | 💭 | `tmp/manager.py:167-174` `get_default_tmp_manager` | **Hidden init side effect via global singleton** - the module-level `_global_tmp_manager` is created lazily on first `get_default_tmp_manager()`/`mktemp()`/`get_temp_file()` call, which constructs a `TmpManager` -> `bootstrap()` (mkdir + chmod) as a non-obvious side effect of an import-time helper. Related to H279 but at the global-API layer. | Make bootstrap explicit (callers opt in) or document that the global helper provisions `/tmp` on first use. |
  
| H288 | 🟡 | `tools/installer.py:21` | **Destructive `shutil.rmtree` with no capability gate** - `install_umer_os` does `shutil.rmtree(dst)` (where `dst = os.path.join(target_dir, src)`) to clear an existing destination before `copytree`, with **no `CapabilityManager` check** (cap-gate family H227/H233/H267/H273); bounded to the fixed allowlisted `src` names under `target_dir`, so 🟡 not 🔴 (contrast `srv/`'s whole-tree 🔴 rmtree). | Gate the delete behind a capability + require explicit confirmation; only ever clear `target_dir`-scoped paths. |
  
| H289 | 🟡 | `tools/installer.py:13` | **Hardcoded default install root `/umer_os`** - `install_umer_os(target_dir="/umer_os")` bakes a Unix absolute path into the signature that ignores env/arg; on any Linux host the default would clobber `/umer_os` unless the caller overrides (H262 family variant: hardcoded path, but a project path not a dev-machine path, contrast srv/tmp H270/H279). | Resolve `target_dir` from env var/arg at runtime; never hardcode a system root as the default. |
  
| H290 | 🟡 | `tools/installer.py:16-22` | **CWD-relative source resolution (wrong-dir copy / fail-open)** - `src` names (`"kernel"`, `"quantum"`, ...) are resolved relative to the current working directory via `os.path.exists(src)` + `shutil.copytree(src, dst)`, not `os.path.dirname(__file__)`/explicit source root; run from the wrong directory it copies whatever is nearby or silently skips (fails open). | Resolve each `src` from the script's own directory or an explicit source-root argument. |
  
| H291 | 🟡 | `tools/installer.py:1-3` | **Missing GPL-3.0 header + `from __future__` (H7 missing-header variant)** - the file opens with only `import os`/`import shutil`/`import logging`; grep found ZERO `GPL`/`Apache`/`Copyright`/`Licence`/`License`/`SPDX` strings AND no `from __future__ import annotations` (like scripts H236 / sbin H232 / sdk H240 / sources H260 / srv H275 / tmp H284). | Add the canonical GPL-3.0 header and `from __future__ import annotations`. |
  
| H292 | 🟡 | `tools/installer.py:10` | **Interactive `input()` EULA with no GPL alignment / real consent** - `show_license()` prints an "AS IS" EULA and blocks on `input("Press ENTER to accept...")`; non-automation-friendly (blocks headless/CI) and the text carries no GPL-3.0 reference / no real consent capture (per authoritative design source the installer EULA is required; this one is minimal + not GPL-aligned). | Add a non-interactive `--accept-eula` flag + GPL-3.0-referencing EULA text. |
  
| H293 | 💭 | `tools/installer.py:22` | **`copytree` fails open (silent partial install)** - a missing source subtree is skipped by the `if os.path.exists(src):` guard with no error/warning surfaced, so the install can complete with entire components absent and the caller is none the wiser. | Raise/surface when an expected source subtree is missing. |
  
| H294 | 💭 | `tools/installer.py:12,17` | **`logging` used without configuration (dropped records)** - `logging.info(...)` is called with no `logging.basicConfig`/handler, so all log lines are silently discarded (observability gap). | Call `logging.basicConfig(...)` once at entry, or use a configured logger. |
  
| H295 | 💭 | `tools/installer.py` (whole module) | **Per-file baseline gaps (no docstring/`__all__`/type hints)** - the module has no module docstring/`__all__`, and `install_umer_os(target_dir="/umer_os")` has no type annotation on `target_dir: str`; minor baseline gap vs the adopted per-file baseline. | Add a module docstring, `__all__`, and type hints on public functions. |
  
| H296 | 🟡 | `usr/sendmail_manager.py:185,195`, `usr/misc_data_manager.py:672,686-739`, `usr/bsd_compat_manager.py:230`, `usr/games_data_manager.py:239`, `usr/xml_manager.py:223` | **Privileged FS ops with no capability gate (cap-gate family)** - `os.symlink`(`/usr/lib/sendmail`)+`os.unlink`(`/usr/sbin/sendmail`) in `sendmail_manager`; `open('w')`+`os.symlink`(magic `/usr/share/misc`->`/usr/share/file/magic`) in `misc_data_manager`; `shutil.rmtree(entry.path)` in `bsd_compat`/`games_data`/`xml` -- all perform privileged writes at system paths with **no `CapabilityManager` check** (cap-gate family H227/H233/H267/H273/H281/H283). | Gate every privileged FS op behind a `CapabilityManager` capability; validate symlink targets; keep `rmtree` bounded to manager-owned paths. |
  
| H297 | 🟡 | `usr/misc_data_manager.py:589`, `usr/bsd_compat_manager.py:101`, `usr/dict_manager.py:171`, `usr/games_data_manager.py:120-121`, `usr/dconf_manager.py:73-75`, `usr/icc_profile_manager.py:111-112`, `usr/nls_manager.py:86`, `usr/ppd_manager.py:110`, `usr/tmac_manager.py:106`, `usr/user_help_manager.py:127`, `usr/terminfo_manager.py:111`, `usr/wayland_manager.py:102-103`, `usr/xdg_manager.py:73-74`, `usr/xml_manager.py:93` | **Construction-time `mkdir` side effects at system paths** - managers call `BASE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` (and sibling dirs) inside `__init__`/`_refresh`, creating `/usr/share/misc`, `/usr/share/dict`, `/usr/share/games`, `/usr/share/dconf`, etc. as a hidden side effect of construction (no cap gate, no opt-in). Like H262/H270/H279 but a system `/usr` root: raises off non-root Linux, creates wrong-place trees off Linux. | Move dir provisioning out of `__init__` behind an explicit `bootstrap()`/capability gate; resolve roots at runtime/arg. |
  
| H298 | 🟡 | `usr/sendmail_manager.py`, `usr/misc_data_manager.py`, `usr/bsd_compat_manager.py:220,235`, `usr/games_data_manager.py:229,244`, `usr/xml_manager.py:213,228` | **Fail-open broad `except Exception` around privileged ops** - privileged symlink/unlink/write/`rmtree` are wrapped in `except Exception: return False`, so a security-relevant failure (e.g. a failed unlink) is silently swallowed and the caller may not check the return. Observability + potential security gap. | Narrow the `except` to expected errors, log + re-raise or return a typed result the caller must check. |
  
| H299 | 🟡 | `usr/` (58 of 61 modules) | **H7 missing GPL-3.0 header cluster (largest in the tree)** - only `kernel_source_manager.py`, `rpm_manager.py`, `source_manager.py` carry any `GPL`/`Copyright`/`SPDX`/`Licence`/`License` string; the other 58 modules ship **no licence header at all** (missing-header variant, like sbin H232 / scripts H236 / sdk H240 / sources H260 / srv H275 / tmp H284 / tools H291). | Add the canonical GPL-3.0 header to all 58 modules. |
  
| H300 | 🟡 | `usr/sendmail_manager.py`, `usr/misc_data_manager.py`, `usr/*` | **Hardcoded absolute `/usr/...` system paths** - class constants bake in `/usr/lib/sendmail`, `/usr/sbin/sendmail`, `/usr/share/misc`, `/usr/share/file/magic`, etc., not resolved from env/arg; combined with import-time `mkdir` (H297) this is non-portable and creates trees in the wrong place off Linux. | Resolve `/usr` roots at runtime/arg; do not create them at import time. |
  
| H301 | 💭 | `usr/lib_manager.py`, `usr/sbin_manager.py`, `usr/rpm_manager.py`, `usr/gcc_manager.py`, `usr/x11_manager.py`, `usr/io_uring.py`, `usr/landlock.py`, `usr/seccomp.py`, `usr/ntsync.py`, `usr/iommufd.py`, `usr/dmabuf.py`, `usr/vduse.py`, `usr/tee.py`, `usr/netlink.py`, `usr/procfs.py`, `usr/sysfs.py`, `usr/syscalls.py` | **SIMULATED privileged/kernel-surface modules masquerade as functional** - the privileged FHS managers (`lib`/`sbin`/`rpm`/`gcc`/`x11`) are in-memory registries with no real syscalls, and the kernel-interface modules define constants/enums/dataclasses but have **NO real syscall wiring** (no `io_uring_setup`/`landlock_create_ruleset`/seccomp/`ioctl`s) - they imply real OS access that does not exist (false-state risk, like sbin H235). | Either wire them to real syscalls behind a cap gate, or clearly label them SIMULATED/EXPERIMENTAL. |
  
| H302 | 💭 | `usr/` (many modules) | **Per-file baseline gaps + no tests** - many modules lack `from __future__ import annotations`/module docstring/`__all__`/type hints, use lazy `import shutil` inside functions (dead at module scope), and `usr/` ships **no `_selftest()`/test module at all** (contrast `srv`/`tmp` which have `test_*.py`). | Add baseline header/docstring/`__all__`/type hints; add a `test_usr.py` exercising LIVE managers against a tmp root. |

| H303 | 🔴 | `var/directory_manager.py:77,87,94,184,106,212`, `var/spool_manager.py:63,86,93,123,130,151,163`, `var/log_manager.py:60,84,127,178,164` | **Path-traversal → arbitrary FS delete/write/RCE (CWE-22)** - `name`/`username`/`directory`/`filename` are joined as `self.<x>_path / param` with no scoping or `realpath`-under-root check; `Path` collapses `..`, so `remove_local_item("../../etc/passwd")`→`unlink("/etc/passwd")`, `VarDirectoryManager.remove_local_item("../etc")`→`shutil.rmtree("/etc")`, and `SpoolManager.set_cron_user("../../etc/cron.d/x", jobs)`→writes `/etc/cron.d/x` (**cron RCE as root**); `LogManager.write_log("../../etc/cron.d/x", …)`→arbitrary append. Read/list variants expose arbitrary file content (`read_mailbox("../../etc/shadow")`). | Validate + normalize every path param (reject `..`/absolute, resolve with `realpath` and assert it stays under the manager root); provide a shared `safe_join(root, name)` helper used by all three managers. ✅ RESOLVED (session 112, **DRIFT**): already remediated — all three managers resolve names via `safe_child`/`safe_join` (`var/_path_guard.py` → canonical `core.path_guard`), fail-closed on `PathTraversalError`, with `[FIX H303]` markers + H304 cap-gates; `tests/test_var.py` H303 regression tests → 32 passed. No unguarded joins remain. |
  
| H304 | 🟡 | `var/directory_manager.py`, `var/spool_manager.py`, `var/log_manager.py` (all privileged FS ops) | **Privileged FS ops with no capability gate (cap-gate family)** - `write_text`/`append`/`unlink`/`rename`/`shutil.rmtree` on `/var/log`,`/var/spool`,`/var/local`,`/var/opt`,`/var/lock`,`/var/run`,`/var/tmp` run with **no `CapabilityManager` check** (family H227/H233/H267/H273/H281/H283/H296). | Gate every privileged FS op behind a `CapabilityManager` capability (e.g. `cap_sys_admin` for spool/cron writes); keep `rmtree` bounded to manager-owned paths. |
  
| H305 | 🟡 | `var/directory_manager.py:81,97,117,130,154,188,218,229,240,259,273,291`, `var/spool_manager.py:99,111,135,165`, `var/log_manager.py:67,91,138,156` | **Fail-open broad `except Exception`** - privileged write/unlink/rmtree/rename are wrapped in `except Exception: return False` (and a bare `except Exception: pass` at `directory_manager.py:154`), so a security-relevant failure is silently swallowed and callers often ignore the return (observability + potential security gap, like usr/ H298 / root H227). | Narrow the `except` to expected errors; log + re-raise or return a typed result the caller must check; never silently `pass`. |
  
| H306 | 🟡 | `var/directory_manager.py:14`, `var/spool_manager.py:13`, `var/log_manager.py:13` | **H7 Apache-2.0 strays (wrong licence declared)** - 3 of 4 modules declare `Licence: GPL-3.0 (GNU General Public License Version 3)`, conflicting with the GPL-3.0 canonical; `__init__.py` carries no licence header at all (opposite of `usr/`'s missing-header H299: here the licence is *present but wrong*). | Add the canonical GPL-3.0 header to all 4 modules (replace the Apache-2.0 lines). |
